This means a care assistance adjusted by support services, which really means a health profession, where people can take care of the sick – exclusive of the domestic activities – and which is as well-trained and as transparent as possible, so that we can not create one-way roads but guarantee a continuous course from low education to tertiary training.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-11-20-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00051_d7e5027	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! ParlaMint-AT_2014-11-20-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00051_d7e5052	Mrs Bundesminister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-11-20-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00051_d7e5067	I also listened to the contribution today in the morning in the “Morgenjournal” with the various councils because the question has been raised: Does that make sense? The answer that, I think, came from Sonja Wehsely can be seen here as an example. We currently have a very, very well-trained health and health care, and we have a level here, which with the Bachelor may be a bit increased, but we assume from a very high level. In training itself, it may be cheaper for the countries, or for the institutions, because the higher education sector is no longer a school. This is one thing when it comes to these budgets. On the question of remuneration afterwards, of course, we try to focus on what is also inside the countries, i.e. the care assistance is the care assistance, which simply gains more time by not including the domestic part in its activities. There is thus more time for training, which may also help to achieve a greater level of competence. The training itself will not change anything as far as the costs are concerned. With regard to the costs incurred by the institutions, I assume that it will be cost-neutral, except, as has been said, the normal increases in some restructuring. ParlaMint-AT_2014-11-20-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00051_d7e7016	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! The government paper under item 1 concerns a larger and two smaller subjects: the further development of the wage and social dumping control law. However, it is also about repairing the Unemployment Insurance Act in connection with ancillary workers and about changes in the field of occupational safety. On all these points, I would like to make one: I would like to make an amendment which will be distributed and which I would like to explain in the course of my speech. On the wage and social dumping law: The development of this law is very important, we very much welcome it. I cannot help but point out that, when the law was passed in 2011, most of the points that are now being included in the amendment were proposed by us in the form of an amendment. But they say yes, better late than never, and so we welcome further development. It will definitely increase the effectiveness of this law if, at least in this area, I am of the opinion of Mrs Belakovich-Jenewein, the appropriate control structures are also strengthened. We need more staff, we need more resources. I therefore make the following request in this context: [[Quoted print version removed]] So we need more staff to increase effectiveness. Despite all the positive steps, this law still has a blind spot; a blind spot that neither the social partners nor the government want to look at nor look at. What's this about? The employee or the employee concerned by underpayment has no guarantee under this law that he/she will ultimately come to the retained money. And why is that the case? – Because the employee concerned is only informed of the procedure when it is over. So the person affected by underpayment is only informed about this with the existence of the criminal decision. What's the problem now? – The problem is that at the same time we have a period of time in the collective agreements, which defines how long after the work has been done one can sue wage. Such a procedure takes at least a few months, but these expiry periods are very short. That is, in practice, this leads the person affected by underpayment to learn only at a time when he has been underpaid to which he can no longer sue. And that's a big manko that's been repaired! [[Congratulation with the greens.]] What do you need for this repair? – An earlier information for those concerned. It is necessary to coordinate the expiry periods with the law in order to ensure that this is so to speak. In our opinion, it would also be useful to introduce an association action, so that not only the individual workers concerned It is also the association, the Chamber of Workers or the union that can sue its under-remuneration. Mr Minister, I think we should look at these suggestions. Perhaps there is the possibility to make further developments in this direction. The second point I would like to address is the changes to the Unemployment Insurance Act. They may have remembered that there was a call, because in the future auxiliary workers would hardly have had the opportunity to obtain unemployment benefit unless they had only a mini mini-mini-mini-good. That is now being repaired, and that is also good. But: How does a self-employed person get him treated differently? I am in favour of the fact that all people who pay into unemployment insurance are entitled to unemployment benefit and receive unemployment benefits in the event of the loss of their jobs, provided that they have no income above the level of poverty. And it doesn't matter what kind of income this is. I do not think it justified to distinguish between whether the additional income comes from agriculture, self-employment or self-employment. This must be treated equally and would also be in line with modern developments in the labour market, because more and more people also have a combination of different forms of activity. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] And finally, the third point: This is about the deterioration in the protection of workers. The ÖVP calls the whole a debureaucratisation, the SPÖ wants to play it down, and at the same time the AUVA – yet a relevant institution in the context of employee protection – strongly criticizes the changes. It does not matter whether a day's work is held in the block or the actual working time including the breaks. It does not matter whether institutions of worker protection meet four times or only once a year. And it does not matter if the trader nominated preventive professionals not only advise them, but also suddenly take over the representation of the workforce. This has consequences, which weakens the protection of workers and, above all, weakens the preventive effect of worker protection, which we want to strengthen. These changes are extremely counterproductive in my understanding. The positive thing about this proposal is still superior, and we will agree to it, although we reject this part with worker protection. I really wish we had more proposals from this government, because the Austrian labour market and employees in Austria would need more positive developments. I hope it continues in this direction.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-11-20-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00051_d7e7215	Madam President. Federal Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen in the High House and also at home in front of the screens! I refer to the legislative package, which essentially includes five areas. Firstly, changes in wage and social dumping. All the components of pay are now included in wage control, so far only the basic wage without overtime and special payments was the standard for wage dumping. We have provided for stricter criminal frameworks. If the wage documents are not provided in the necessary form, we have at present Penalties from at least €500 to a maximum of € 5 000, we increase them to € 1 000 or max. € 10 000. However, we have also changed the so-called bagatll limit from 3 percent to 10 percent, according to the principle of “consultation instead of punishment” in minor submissions. That is to say, in the case of an unintended little compromise, not the full punishment should apply. The “consultation rather than punishment” principle is implemented here, but to protect the domestic economy and also the jobs associated with it, these changes are being carried out in this area, which we consider very necessary. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The second point – I do not understand the criticism as a works council – is that: We have facilitation of working time records. It is also not pleasant for workers if they always have to print the same paper on fixed working hours. There is a meaningful change: In the case of fixed working hours, the recording may be complete, only the deviations must be recorded. This means less paper and less time in the sense of the service providers and servants. The recording of rest breaks may also be more frequent, so far it was limited by 30 minutes. This is also covered by the title of simplification, ladies and gentlemen. In return, workers receive the right to submit the working time record once a month upon request. For companies, the reporting obligation on shift work and short-time work against the labour inspector is still missing. This is debureaucratisation and simplification. Many entrepreneurs are moaning in this area, which is always told us on business visits. That is why we consider this area to be very useful, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Third point, debureaucratisation: I do not understand why one cannot agree with that in the area of the Commissioners, as there is really no over-targeting. The fire protection groups and the mandatory meetings of the Safety Committee are reduced from two to one per year. Of course, one third of the members of the Committee may convene a meeting at any time. The function of preventive professionals and security trustees is also simplified, which can then be carried out by a person. In any case, this will make it easier for businesses and not at all any disadvantage for workers. That is why this point will, in any case, also be approved. [[Congratulation with the ÖVP and the Abg. Kuzdas.]] The fourth point concerns legal certainty for auxiliary farmers in relation to unemployment benefits. I would like to thank our Federal Minister, Mr Hundstorfer, in particular, for it is not a matter of course that this is implemented so quickly. In a very short time, the legal situation in force until the summer was re-established, namely that the relevant unit value, based on the low flexibility limit, was again €13 177. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a very important point for many thousands of other farmers. Mr Federal Minister, I would also like to thank you for the letter that has already been received to the AMS posts in this context. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] The last point: We know we have a tense situation on the labour market. In addition, 30 million will be made available for short-term work, because it is necessary to counteract a weaker economy. This is another point. It's done here. Overall, the main points for the labour market are also simplification and debureaucratisation. Mein Ladies and gentlemen, this package is approved by us, and I also ask you to agree to this law.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-11-20-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00051_d7e11160	Mr President. Estimated Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Dear spectators in the plenary room and on TV screens! At this item on the agenda, we will decide on an all-party proposal to make a scientific study together with the public prosecutor on the issue of abuse and violence to people with disabilities. I think this is a very important decision that we are going to take today so that we will finally have data and facts on violence against people with disabilities in Austria. Violence is still a taboo theme, ladies and gentlemen, it doesn't matter whether it is violence against women – although I think we have taken many measures in recent years to get this issue out of the taboo zone; we also have far-reaching anti-violence laws in the field of violence against women – or violence against children. The so-called g’sunde Watsch’n is somehow back in the mouth, which I find very much to be debateable especially on today’s children’s rights day. I don't think there are the g’sundes Wach’n for children exactly a hundred percent, and on the other hand we should all appear. [[In the case of the SPÖ, representatives of the Greens and the European Parliament, the Committee of the Regions and the Economic and Social Committee, the Committee of the Regions and the Economic and Social Committee, the ……]] Of course, it is also a very special taboo issue when it comes to violence against people with disabilities. There's a double taboo: The impairment is still a matter which is not completely naturally being discussed and, of course, violence against people with disabilities. That is why we should all take joint action to get this issue out of the taboo zone and to reach the public's point of view. I am sure that the study will contribute to this. Violence, ladies and gentlemen, occurs in various forms. There is physical violence, which, as I think, is easily recognizable, for outsiders and also for someone who is affected by physical violence. You know, you're a victim of violence. But there is also the psychological violence, which is much more subtle, which often lasts for years, where as a victim one often does not know whether one is actually affected by psychological violence. These are insults, abuses, degradations and also ignorances; even ignorance can actually be psychological violence. People with disabilities are often affected by this so-called psychological subtle violence because they often need assistance for a lifetime, whether from family members or institutions, because they need support and support in their everyday lives. This dependency ratio is often also a reason why people with disabilities are affected by violence. I think you really have to look, you have to strengthen people with disabilities, to recognize yourself as victims, to be able to articulate and to say: No, here's my limit, there's just the limit where you're crossing my intimate atmosphere! Or to say where it's actually psychological violence. That is why it is important for us to address this issue, which is why this study is particularly important. The Monitoring Committee, which monitors the Conventions on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities in Austria, has also discussed this issue at a public meeting and has pointed out very clearly that it is important on the one hand to collect data so that we finally know what we are talking about, and on the other hand to take measures so that people with disabilities can actually be strengthened in their perception and also strengthen that they can simply stop saying so. I am pleased that we will take this first step together today at the initiative of Mr Jarmer to commission this study and I am sure that this will also contribute to bringing violence to people with disabilities and sexual abuse to the public. Of course, it would be even more beautiful if we all fought against it together, so that this violence is stopped.

The National Action Plan on the Protection of Women from Violence comprises a bundle of 64 measures. These are a very good co-examination from different resorts. The measures will be implemented by the Federal Government in the years 2014 to 2016. A focus here is on prevention, i.e. prevention that the crime does not happen at all. Austria is an international pioneer in the field of anti-violence. We have a good network of anti-violence institutions and also many protection laws, but we want to get even better. The new action plan shows that we can still do something, and it also shows where to close gaps. An example of the action plan for prevention is the campaign that started in September, “violence of FREI” and a guide for hospital staff. In the hospital, among other things, victims are looking for medical advice, and this guide is intended to help ensure that hospital staff can make the right diagnosis. The NAP, i.e. the National Action Plan, also provides for greater cooperation with NGOs and federal states in collecting and preparing data from violent cases. Every fifth woman in Austria will be at least in her life. the victims of violence. Unfortunately, the Austrian women know too little about aid offers. One of the objectives of the NAP is therefore to make already existing help offers such as the women's helpline or the fem:HELP app better known. In order to identify risk families and potential stakeholders early on, pilot projects will be available via the local health telephone in selected regions. In the area of material rights, i.e. criminal law in this context, we have set ourselves the goal of re-evaluating crimes against body and life as well as against sexual integrity. Because it cannot be that these are being disproportionately persecuted against property offences. In the last ten years, we have – the statistics of the Federal Asyl Office – everyone can look up an asylum rate of 20 percent. That is, of 100 percent who apply, 20 percent receive asylum, on average, because they have real reasons, are persecuted, come out of a war or find other reasons. 80 per cent do not receive it, and this is an established number, by the way, which also stems from the answer to one of the questions put to the Minister on our part. Another value: 72 percent of all asylum seekers are men, which can also be read, mainly between 18 and 30 years. Also a very important number is – it was difficult to find out, but it is in the budget; this is for all those who have not read it –: A negative first instance decision takes exactly 48 days in Austria. 48 days, this can be read in the budget. In the case of the first instance judgments, 85 percent will fall within six months – a number that you are expected to know – and now I come to another interesting point: If someone gets a negative asylum notice and goes into other instances, it can take a long time, I'll give you the right. However, I would point to a value that you also find in the budget: In the second instance, those who go along this path receive a negative 95 percent notice. All of this is clearly defined in the budget. So, in the second instance, 95 percent is not granted asylum again. Now we are coming to work – this is very important and this is also known to the least, especially at NEOS: If someone is an asylum seekers, he can be independent from the first day. You don't have to wait three months, not at all. As with Austrians: If they comply with the trade regulations, they can work independently from the first day. It's just a fact. I just want to bring this to your attention. [[Abg. Strolz: But the company's start-up is not easy in Austria!]] In Austria, after a waiting period of three months, there is an admission for eight teaching occupations, i.e. asylum seekers also have the opportunity to take eight teaching occupations. Then there are job shortages, which is a list of 25 occupations: Baker, cook, taper, everything possible. These professions can also be taken immediately after three months. [[Abg. Schwentner: This is different from federal to federal!]] The possibility of working as seasonal workers and harvest workers also exists, quite clearly, and the possibility of working profitably, at 3,50 € per hour at the community, also nothing new. For example, there is a project in Tyrol, that is, AKI, from the Chamber of Workers and the AMS. With this, 107 asylum seekers have received a Buddy, one of the economy, one of the AMS who has tried to integrate these people into the world of work. You have to imagine the effort. The result was: 56 percent have not managed to enter the labour market despite these two buddies, saying they have never begun to enter the labour market. And of the 44 percent who made it thanks to this massive aid – which would wish for 400,000 unemployed in Austria – 90 percent have again achieved the rise in work as an auxiliary worker. ParlaMint-AT_2014-11-20-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00051_d7e19930	Mr President. Minister! If we now ratify the Valletta Convention for the Protection of the Archaeological Heritage – only 22 years after its establishment, but after all – then we do not start at zero in Austria, but we can build on good conditions. As part of the EIA, there is already an obligation to include existing archaeological sites in the planning already in the run-up to the implementation of major infrastructure projects. I think there is a high sensitivity to these issues in Austria. For example, in Upper Austria the renovation or renovation of the country house in Linz has experienced some delay, because in the course of the reconstruction measures old foundations were laid out, which are now visible to the public, and on the other hand, there have been skeletal finds that had to be properly classified. So there is a high sensitivity and also a high willingness to understand and preserve your own history as a learning place. One point that is particularly important for the federal states is the question of the impact that this has on land use plans. To this end, there is a kind of cataster about archaeological sites, regardless of whether these sites already exist in real life or are only suspected by scientists. This cataster is then to be incorporated into the spatial planning plans or into the resulting spatial planning concepts. The aim is to ensure that these two important issues do not cross-border and that one keeps one from the other's opposite interests from the outset. I would say that there are two positive requests, and I therefore request broad approval. Thank you.

At the same time, there is an individual and collective responsibility regarding the cultural heritage. The Council of Europe's Framework Convention on the Value of Cultural Heritage for Society, which we are ratifying today, takes up this idea and says that these are very important, current issues. [[President Hofer shall take over the chairmanship.]] An example should be given here: For years, the trend has been to observe that works of art are becoming more and more speculation objects and that pioneering works of painting are taking place in art depots instead of being accessible to all in museums. Sustainable use of the cultural heritage, as required in the agreement, is something quite different. Important issues such as promoting interdisciplinary research and using digital technologies are also addressed in order to make major works of art even easier for all people. It should also be noted that the objectives set out in the Framework Convention, such as promoting cultural diversity, intercultural dialogue and cultural participation, are also central to Austrian cultural policy. It is therefore consistent and welcome that Austria, together with the European Convention on the Protection of Archaeological Heritage, is also very pleased to ratify this Framework Convention. Ladies and gentlemen! Soil monuments are today exposed to many dangers, whether by robbery or pollution. It is all the more important that Austria actively strives to protect this important heritage. Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! With the ratification of both agreements, Austria will once again respect its reputation as a cultural nation.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-11-20-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00051_d7e22257	Mr President. Minister! My dear! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to make a few points on the subject of control equipment. This is, as has already been mentioned, a legal implementation of a social partners agreement for the benefit of many, especially for the benefit of workers, but also for employers in the sense of red tape simplification. On the one hand, existing exceptions for bus buses are being extended or extended. It is new that high-speed somnibuses are now excluded from this control device obligation. Ladies and gentlemen! The reasons for this are in practice, obvious and comprehensible. Without these exceptions, for example, An additional entry shall be made to the control device for a change of handlebar. This takes – we know from practice – usually five, six or even often seven minutes. Imagine, in cities with a good public, densely-connected transport network, such as Vienna, Salzburg, but also Linz, seven minutes in addition to the bus stop or even on a lane for general traffic. That's exactly what we're preventing by this exception. Or: Similarly, the daily production of a form sheet for each handlebar/manufacturer, which must be completed, signed and carried in the original form before each journey. This makes sense in long-distance transport, not in local transport. Therefore, this exception. However, it is also clear that this exception applies only on condition that the corresponding records are located in the establishment. This means that controls on the protection of workers – and this is a very important point for trade unions in particular – will not be affected. In summary, this is a sensible, practical agreement, and I ask for approval.

We have these derogations, especially for small-scale operations – this is not about the transport of goods, but about the transport of goods needed for the work. It is only necessary to imagine an installer that is driving to a repair site et cetera, and the regulation is that he also does not have to incorporate these control devices into his car. I am particularly pleased that we have also created this derogation for agriculture, where we have live animals transports that we could go from 50 to 100 km. This too means simplification. But it is also very important to me, because this was a criticism in the Committee, to point out that on the one hand, it does not harm regionality if you extend the radius from 50 to 100 km, because not every journey with living animals is, so to speak, the last trip to a slaughterhouse, but you also deal with animals, it is often the drive to an alm, et cetera. Therefore, the radius of 50 km is too limited. It's a relief to go there at 100 km. The second is something very essential, which is a concern for all of us: animal welfare. Also animal welfare is not attacked. Carriers who transport animals, or farmers who transport animals, continue to have to fill in animal transport tickets, with the time of transport, how much piece and, of course, the distance in kilometres. [[Abg. Riemer: There are most objections! Over 300 out of 1,000 checks! It's bad!]] Yes, Mr President, I know that you are always talking about making things easier for farmers. Yesterday we had the request that we should shorten the storage periods for records, papers for ÖPUL. I say to you: It is probably the smaller pain for a farmer if he has some paper on the attic for three years than if he has to buy, install and then use great control devices every time he has to carry something. I think that animal welfare is enough here. If your kind of control is that we not only have to give two order marks to each cow in the ears, but in the end we also need to give every farmer a brand so that he is decently controlled, then that is not our line. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Riemer]] I am very glad that we are in the government and not you!

We can only review these objectives if we also have the relevant data. The present resolution will ensure this data. That is why this proposal is a very good and very supportive one. – Thank you very much.

We can report that the number of arrivals increased by around 2.3 percent between May and September. The final settlement results will only be published in 10 days. This results in a record value of 17.94 million arrivals – actually sensational for the current situation!
